^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  143) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  144)     The declared parameters are re-shuffled depending on the order in which
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  145)     they were invoked, as well as the eagerness of each parameters.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  146)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  148)     order in which the user provided them to the CLI is respected.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  149) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  150)     This behavior and its effect on callback evaluation is detailed at:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  151)     https://click.palletsprojects.com/en/stable/advanced/#callback-evaluation-order
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  152)     """

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  155)         try:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  156)             idx: float = invocation_order.index(item)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  157)         except ValueError:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  158)             idx = float("inf")

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  160)         return not item.is_eager, idx

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  203) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  204) class Context:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  205)     """The context is a special internal object that holds state relevant
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  206)     for the script execution at every single level.  It's normally invisible
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  207)     to commands unless they opt-in to getting access to it.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  208) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  209)     The context is useful as it can pass internal objects around and can
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  210)     control special execution features such as reading data from
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  211)     environment variables.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  212) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  213)     A context can be used as context manager in which case it will call
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  214)     :meth:`close` on teardown.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  215) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  216)     :param command: the command class for this context.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  217)     :param parent: the parent context.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  218)     :param info_name: the info name for this invocation.  Generally this
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  219)                       is the most descriptive name for the script or
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  220)                       command.  For the toplevel script it is usually
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  221)                       the name of the script, for commands below that it's
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  222)                       the name of the script.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  223)     :param obj: an arbitrary object of user data.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  224)     :param auto_envvar_prefix: the prefix to use for automatic environment
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  225)                                variables.  If this is `None` then reading
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  226)                                from environment variables is disabled.  This
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  227)                                does not affect manually set environment
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  228)                                variables which are always read.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  229)     :param default_map: a dictionary (like object) with default values
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  230)                         for parameters.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  231)     :param terminal_width: the width of the terminal.  The default is
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  232)                            inherit from parent context.  If no context
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  233)                            defines the terminal width then auto
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  234)                            detection will be applied.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  235)     :param max_content_width: the maximum width for content rendered by
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  236)                               Click (this currently only affects help
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  237)                               pages).  This defaults to 80 characters if
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  238)                               not overridden.  In other words: even if the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  239)                               terminal is larger than that, Click will not
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  240)                               format things wider than 80 characters by
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  241)                               default.  In addition to that, formatters might
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  242)                               add some safety mapping on the right.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  243)     :param resilient_parsing: if this flag is enabled then Click will
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  244)                               parse without any interactivity or callback
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  245)                               invocation.  Default values will also be
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  246)                               ignored.  This is useful for implementing
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  247)                               things such as completion support.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  248)     :param allow_extra_args: if this is set to `True` then extra arguments
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  249)                              at the end will not raise an error and will be
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  250)                              kept on the context.  The default is to inherit
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  251)                              from the command.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  252)     :param allow_interspersed_args: if this is set to `False` then options
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  253)                                     and arguments cannot be mixed.  The
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  254)                                     default is to inherit from the command.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  255)     :param ignore_unknown_options: instructs click to ignore options it does
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  256)                                    not know and keeps them for later
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  257)                                    processing.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  258)     :param help_option_names: optionally a list of strings that define how
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  259)                               the default help parameter is named.  The
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  260)                               default is ``['--help']``.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  261)     :param token_normalize_func: an optional function that is used to
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  262)                                  normalize tokens (options, choices,
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  263)                                  etc.).  This for instance can be used to
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  264)                                  implement case insensitive behavior.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  265)     :param color: controls if the terminal supports ANSI colors or not.  The
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  266)                   default is autodetection.  This is only needed if ANSI
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  267)                   codes are used in texts that Click prints which is by
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  268)                   default not the case.  This for instance would affect
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  269)                   help output.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  270)     :param show_default: Show the default value for commands. If this
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  271)         value is not set, it defaults to the value from the parent
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  272)         context. ``Command.show_default`` overrides this default for the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  273)         specific command.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  274) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  275)     .. versionchanged:: 8.2
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  276)         The ``protected_args`` attribute is deprecated and will be removed in
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  277)         Click 9.0. ``args`` will contain remaining unparsed tokens.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  278) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  279)     .. versionchanged:: 8.1
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  280)         The ``show_default`` parameter is overridden by
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  281)         ``Command.show_default``, instead of the other way around.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  282) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  283)     .. versionchanged:: 8.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  284)         The ``show_default`` parameter defaults to the value from the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  285)         parent context.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  286) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  287)     .. versionchanged:: 7.1
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  288)        Added the ``show_default`` parameter.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  289) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  290)     .. versionchanged:: 4.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  291)         Added the ``color``, ``ignore_unknown_options``, and
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  292)         ``max_content_width`` parameters.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  293) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  294)     .. versionchanged:: 3.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  295)         Added the ``allow_extra_args`` and ``allow_interspersed_args``
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  296)         parameters.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  297) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  298)     .. versionchanged:: 2.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  299)         Added the ``resilient_parsing``, ``help_option_names``, and
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  300)         ``token_normalize_func`` parameters.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  301)     """

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  391) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  392)         #: This flag indicates if a subcommand is going to be executed. A
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  393)         #: group callback can use this information to figure out if it's
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  394)         #: being executed directly or because the execution flow passes
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  395)         #: onwards to a subcommand. By default it's None, but it can be
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  396)         #: the name of the subcommand to execute.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  397)         #:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  398)         #: If chaining is enabled this will be set to ``'*'`` in case
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  399)         #: any commands are executed.  It is however not possible to
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  400)         #: figure out which ones.  If you require this knowledge you
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  401)         #: should use a :func:`result_callback`.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  402)         self.invoked_subcommand = None

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  411)             max_content_width = parent.max_content_width

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  422)         #:

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  427)             allow_interspersed_args = command.allow_interspersed_args
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  428) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  429)         #: Indicates if the context allows mixing of arguments and
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  430)         #: options or not.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  431)         #:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  432)         #: .. versionadded:: 3.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  433)         self.allow_interspersed_args = allow_interspersed_args
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  434) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  435)         if ignore_unknown_options is None:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  436)             ignore_unknown_options = command.ignore_unknown_options
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  437) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  438)         #: Instructs click to ignore options that a command does not
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  439)         #: understand and will store it on the context for later
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  440)         #: processing.  This is primarily useful for situations where you
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  441)         #: want to call into external programs.  Generally this pattern is
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  442)         #: strongly discouraged because it's not possibly to losslessly

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  564)     @contextmanager
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  565)     def scope(self, cleanup: bool = True) -> cabc.Generator[Context]:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  566)         """This helper method can be used with the context object to promote
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  567)         it to the current thread local (see :func:`get_current_context`).
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  568)         The default behavior of this is to invoke the cleanup functions which
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  569)         can be disabled by setting `cleanup` to `False`.  The cleanup
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  570)         functions are typically used for things such as closing file handles.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  571) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  572)         If the cleanup is intended the context object can also be directly
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  573)         used as a context manager.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  574) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  575)         Example usage::
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  576) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  577)             with ctx.scope():
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  578)                 assert get_current_context() is ctx
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  579) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  580)         This is equivalent::
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  581) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  582)             with ctx:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  583)                 assert get_current_context() is ctx
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  584) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  585)         .. versionadded:: 5.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  586) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  587)         :param cleanup: controls if the cleanup functions should be run or
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  588)                         not.  The default is to run these functions.  In
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  589)                         some situations the context only wants to be
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  590)                         temporarily pushed in which case this can be disabled.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  591)                         Nested pushes automatically defer the cleanup.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  592)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  593)         if not cleanup:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  594)             self._depth += 1
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  595)         try:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  596)             with self as rv:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  597)                 yield rv
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  598)         finally:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  599)             if not cleanup:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  600)                 self._depth -= 1
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  601) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  602)     @property
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  603)     def meta(self) -> dict[str, t.Any]:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  604)         """This is a dictionary which is shared with all the contexts
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  605)         that are nested.  It exists so that click utilities can store some
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  606)         state here if they need to.  It is however the responsibility of
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  607)         that code to manage this dictionary well.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  608) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  609)         The keys are supposed to be unique dotted strings.  For instance
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  610)         module paths are a good choice for it.  What is stored in there is
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  611)         irrelevant for the operation of click.  However what is important is
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  612)         that code that places data here adheres to the general semantics of
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  613)         the system.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  614) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  615)         Example usage::
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  616) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  617)             LANG_KEY = f'{__name__}.lang'
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  618) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  619)             def set_language(value):
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  620)                 ctx = get_current_context()
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  621)                 ctx.meta[LANG_KEY] = value
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  622) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  623)             def get_language():
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  624)                 return get_current_context().meta.get(LANG_KEY, 'en_US')
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  625) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  626)         .. versionadded:: 5.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  627)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  628)         return self._meta
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  629) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  630)     def make_formatter(self) -> HelpFormatter:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  631)         """Creates the :class:`~click.HelpFormatter` for the help and
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  632)         usage output.

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  644)     def with_resource(self, context_manager: AbstractContextManager[V]) -> V:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  645)         """Register a resource as if it were used in a ``with``
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  646)         statement. The resource will be cleaned up when the context is
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  647)         popped.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  648) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  649)         Uses :meth:`contextlib.ExitStack.enter_context`. It calls the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  650)         resource's ``__enter__()`` method and returns the result. When
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  651)         the context is popped, it closes the stack, which calls the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  652)         resource's ``__exit__()`` method.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  653) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  654)         To register a cleanup function for something that isn't a
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  655)         context manager, use :meth:`call_on_close`. Or use something
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  656)         from :mod:`contextlib` to turn it into a context manager first.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  657) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  658)         .. code-block:: python
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  659) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  660)             @click.group()
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  661)             @click.option("--name")
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  662)             @click.pass_context
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  663)             def cli(ctx):
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  664)                 ctx.obj = ctx.with_resource(connect_db(name))
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  665)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  711)     def command_path(self) -> str:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  712)         """The computed command path.  This is used for the ``usage``
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  713)         information on the help page.  It's automatically created by

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  715)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  716)         rv = ""
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  717)         if self.info_name is not None:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  718)             rv = self.info_name
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  719)         if self.parent is not None:

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  725) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  726)             rv = f"{' '.join(parent_command_path)} {rv}"
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  727)         return rv.lstrip()

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  729)     def find_root(self) -> Context:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  730)         """Finds the outermost context."""
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  731)         node = self
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  732)         while node.parent is not None:

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  739) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  740)         while node is not None:

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  763)         """

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  765)             name is not None

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  837)     def _make_sub_context(self, command: Command) -> Context:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  838)         """Create a new context of the same type as this context, but

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  846)     def invoke(
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  847)         self, callback: t.Callable[..., V], /, *args: t.Any, **kwargs: t.Any
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  848)     ) -> V: ...
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  849) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  850)     @t.overload
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  851)     def invoke(self, callback: Command, /, *args: t.Any, **kwargs: t.Any) -> t.Any: ...
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  852) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  853)     def invoke(
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  854)         self, callback: Command | t.Callable[..., V], /, *args: t.Any, **kwargs: t.Any
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  855)     ) -> t.Any | V:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  856)         """Invokes a command callback in exactly the way it expects.  There
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  857)         are two ways to invoke this method:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  858) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  859)         1.  the first argument can be a callback and all other arguments and
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  860)             keyword arguments are forwarded directly to the function.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  861)         2.  the first argument is a click command object.  In that case all
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  862)             arguments are forwarded as well but proper click parameters
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  863)             (options and click arguments) must be keyword arguments and Click
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  864)             will fill in defaults.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  865) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  866)         .. versionchanged:: 8.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  867)             All ``kwargs`` are tracked in :attr:`params` so they will be
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  868)             passed if :meth:`forward` is called at multiple levels.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  869) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  870)         .. versionchanged:: 3.2
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  871)             A new context is created, and missing arguments use default values.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  872)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  873)         if isinstance(callback, Command):
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  874)             other_cmd = callback
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  875) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  876)             if other_cmd.callback is None:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  877)                 raise TypeError(
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  878)                     "The given command does not have a callback that can be invoked."
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  879)                 )
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  880)             else:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  881)                 callback = t.cast("t.Callable[..., V]", other_cmd.callback)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  882) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  883)             ctx = self._make_sub_context(other_cmd)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  884) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  885)             for param in other_cmd.params:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  886)                 if param.name not in kwargs and param.expose_value:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  887)                     default_value = param.get_default(ctx)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  888)                     # We explicitly hide the :attr:`UNSET` value to the user, as we
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  889)                     # choose to make it an implementation detail. And because ``invoke``
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  890)                     # has been designed as part of Click public API, we return ``None``
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  891)                     # instead. Refs:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  892)                     # https://github.com/pallets/click/issues/3066
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  893)                     # https://github.com/pallets/click/issues/3065
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  894)                     # https://github.com/pallets/click/pull/3068
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  895)                     if default_value is UNSET:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  896)                         default_value = None
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  897)                     kwargs[param.name] = param.type_cast_value(ctx, default_value)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  898) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  899)             # Track all kwargs as params, so that forward() will pass
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  900)             # them on in subsequent calls.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  901)             ctx.params.update(kwargs)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  902)         else:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  903)             ctx = self
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  904) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  905)         with augment_usage_errors(self):
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  906)             with ctx:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  907)                 return callback(*args, **kwargs)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  908) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  909)     def forward(self, cmd: Command, /, *args: t.Any, **kwargs: t.Any) -> t.Any:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  910)         """Similar to :meth:`invoke` but fills in default keyword
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  911)         arguments from the current context if the other command expects
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  912)         it.  This cannot invoke callbacks directly, only other commands.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  913) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  914)         .. versionchanged:: 8.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  915)             All ``kwargs`` are tracked in :attr:`params` so they will be
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  916)             passed if ``forward`` is called at multiple levels.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  917)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  918)         # Can only forward to other commands, not direct callbacks.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  919)         if not isinstance(cmd, Command):
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  920)             raise TypeError("Callback is not a command.")
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  921) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  922)         for param in self.params:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  923)             if param not in kwargs:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  924)                 kwargs[param] = self.params[param]
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  925) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  926)         return self.invoke(cmd, *args, **kwargs)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  927) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  928)     def set_parameter_source(self, name: str, source: ParameterSource) -> None:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  929)         """Set the source of a parameter. This indicates the location
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  930)         from which the value of the parameter was obtained.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  931) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  932)         :param name: The name of the parameter.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  933)         :param source: A member of :class:`~click.core.ParameterSource`.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  934)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  935)         self._parameter_source[name] = source
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  936) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  937)     def get_parameter_source(self, name: str) -> ParameterSource | None:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  938)         """Get the source of a parameter. This indicates the location
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  939)         from which the value of the parameter was obtained.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  940) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  941)         This can be useful for determining when a user specified a value
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  942)         on the command line that is the same as the default value. It
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  943)         will be :attr:`~click.core.ParameterSource.DEFAULT` only if the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  944)         value was actually taken from the default.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  945) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  946)         :param name: The name of the parameter.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  947)         :rtype: ParameterSource
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  948) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  949)         .. versionchanged:: 8.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  950)             Returns ``None`` if the parameter was not provided from any
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  951)             source.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  952)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  953)         return self._parameter_source.get(name)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  954) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  955) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  956) class Command:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  957)     """Commands are the basic building block of command line interfaces in
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  958)     Click.  A basic command handles command line parsing and might dispatch
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  959)     more parsing to commands nested below it.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  960) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  961)     :param name: the name of the command to use unless a group overrides it.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  962)     :param context_settings: an optional dictionary with defaults that are
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  963)                              passed to the context object.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  964)     :param callback: the callback to invoke.  This is optional.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  965)     :param params: the parameters to register with this command.  This can
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  966)                    be either :class:`Option` or :class:`Argument` objects.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  967)     :param help: the help string to use for this command.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  968)     :param epilog: like the help string but it's printed at the end of the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  969)                    help page after everything else.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  970)     :param short_help: the short help to use for this command.  This is
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  971)                        shown on the command listing of the parent command.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  972)     :param add_help_option: by default each command registers a ``--help``
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  973)                             option.  This can be disabled by this parameter.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  974)     :param no_args_is_help: this controls what happens if no arguments are
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  975)                             provided.  This option is disabled by default.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  976)                             If enabled this will add ``--help`` as argument
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  977)                             if no arguments are passed
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  978)     :param hidden: hide this command from help outputs.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  979)     :param deprecated: If ``True`` or non-empty string, issues a message
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  980)                         indicating that the command is deprecated and highlights
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  981)                         its deprecation in --help. The message can be customized
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  982)                         by using a string as the value.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  983) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  984)     .. versionchanged:: 8.2
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  985)         This is the base class for all commands, not ``BaseCommand``.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  986)         ``deprecated`` can be set to a string as well to customize the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  987)         deprecation message.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  988) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  989)     .. versionchanged:: 8.1
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  990)         ``help``, ``epilog``, and ``short_help`` are stored unprocessed,
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  991)         all formatting is done when outputting help text, not at init,
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  992)         and is done even if not using the ``@command`` decorator.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  993) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  994)     .. versionchanged:: 8.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  995)         Added a ``repr`` showing the command name.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  996) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  997)     .. versionchanged:: 7.1
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  998)         Added the ``no_args_is_help`` parameter.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200  999) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1000)     .. versionchanged:: 2.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1001)         Added the ``context_settings`` parameter.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1002)     """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1003) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1004)     #: The context class to create with :meth:`make_context`.

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1006)     #: .. versionadded:: 8.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1007)     context_class: type[Context] = Context
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1008) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1009)     #: the default for the :attr:`Context.allow_extra_args` flag.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1010)     allow_extra_args = False
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1011)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1013)     allow_interspersed_args = True
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1014)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1089) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1090)     def get_usage(self, ctx: Context) -> str:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1091)         """Formats the usage line into a string and returns it.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1092) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1093)         Calls :meth:`format_usage` internally.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1094)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1095)         formatter = ctx.make_formatter()
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1096)         self.format_usage(ctx, formatter)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1272)     ) -> Context:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1273)         """This function when given an info name and arguments will kick
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1274)         off the parsing and create a new :class:`Context`.  It does not
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1275)         invoke the actual command callback though.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1276) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1277)         To quickly customize the context class used without overriding

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1279) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1280)         :param info_name: the info name for this invocation.  Generally this
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1281)                           is the most descriptive name for the script or
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1282)                           command.  For the toplevel script it's usually
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1283)                           the name of the script, for commands below it's
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1284)                           the name of the command.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1285)         :param args: the arguments to parse as list of strings.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1286)         :param parent: the parent context if available.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1287)         :param extra: extra keyword arguments forwarded to the context

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1289) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1290)         .. versionchanged:: 8.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1291)             Added the :attr:`context_class` attribute.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1292)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1293)         for key, value in self.context_settings.items():
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1294)             if key not in extra:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1295)                 extra[key] = value
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1296) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1297)         ctx = self.context_class(self, info_name=info_name, parent=parent, **extra)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1298) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1299)         with ctx.scope(cleanup=False):
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1300)             self.parse_args(ctx, args)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1301)         return ctx
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1302) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1303)     def parse_args(self, ctx: Context, args: list[str]) -> list[str]:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1304)         if not args and self.no_args_is_help and not ctx.resilient_parsing:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1305)             raise NoArgsIsHelpError(ctx)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1306)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1309)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1311)             _, args = param.handle_parse_result(ctx, opts, args)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1312) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1313)         # We now have all parameters' values into `ctx.params`, but the data may contain
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1314)         # the `UNSET` sentinel.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1315)         # Convert `UNSET` to `None` to ensure that the user doesn't see `UNSET`.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1316)         #
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1317)         # Waiting until after the initial parse to convert allows us to treat `UNSET`
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1318)         # more like a missing value when multiple params use the same name.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1319)         # Refs:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1320)         # https://github.com/pallets/click/issues/3071
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1321)         # https://github.com/pallets/click/pull/3079
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1322)         for name, value in ctx.params.items():
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1323)             if value is UNSET:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1324)                 ctx.params[name] = None
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1325) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1326)         if args and not ctx.allow_extra_args and not ctx.resilient_parsing:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1327)             ctx.fail(
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1328)                 ngettext(
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1329)                     "Got unexpected extra argument ({args})",
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1330)                     "Got unexpected extra arguments ({args})",
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1331)                     len(args),
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1332)                 ).format(args=" ".join(map(str, args)))
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1333)             )
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1334) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1335)         ctx.args = args

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1337)         return args
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1338)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1340)         """Given a context, this invokes the attached callback (if it exists)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1341)         in the right way.

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1431)         """This is the way to invoke a script with all the bells and
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1432)         whistles as a command line application.  This will always terminate
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1433)         the application after a call.  If this is not wanted, ``SystemExit``
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1434)         needs to be caught.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1435) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1436)         This method is also available by directly calling the instance of
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1437)         a :class:`Command`.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1438)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1441)         :param prog_name: the program name that should be used.  By default

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1443)                           name from ``sys.argv[0]``.

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1445)                              bash completion support.  The default is
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1446)                              ``"_<prog_name>_COMPLETE"`` with prog_name in

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1461) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1462)         .. versionchanged:: 8.0.1
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1463)             Added the ``windows_expand_args`` parameter to allow
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1464)             disabling command line arg expansion on Windows.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1465)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1468)             patterns, user dir, and env vars are expanded.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1469) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1470)         .. versionchanged:: 3.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1471)            Added the ``standalone_mode`` parameter.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1472)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1473)         if args is None:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1474)             args = sys.argv[1:]
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1475) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1476)             if os.name == "nt" and windows_expand_args:

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1513)                     sys.exit(1)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1514)                 else:

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1519)             else:

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1521)                 # note that this is only reached if `self.invoke` above raises
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1522)                 # an Exit explicitly -- thus bypassing the check there which

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1526)                 # `ctx.exit(1)` and to `return 1`, the caller won't be able to
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1527)                 # tell the difference between the two
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1528)                 return e.exit_code
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1529)         except Abort:

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1531)                 raise

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1533)             sys.exit(1)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1534) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1535)     def _main_shell_completion(

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1588)     """A group is a command that nests other commands (or more groups).
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1589) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1590)     :param name: The name of the group command.

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1719)     def add_command(self, cmd: Command, name: str | None = None) -> None:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1720)         """Registers another :class:`Command` with this group.  If the name
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1721)         is not provided, the name of the command is used.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1722)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1723)         name = name or cmd.name
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1724)         if name is None:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1725)             raise TypeError("Command has no name.")

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1727)         self.commands[name] = cmd

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1739)     ) -> t.Callable[[t.Callable[..., t.Any]], Command] | Command:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1740)         """A shortcut decorator for declaring and attaching a command to
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1741)         the group. This takes the same arguments as :func:`command` and
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1742)         immediately registers the created command with this group by
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1743)         calling :meth:`add_command`.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1744)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1746)         :attr:`command_class` attribute.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1747)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1749)             This decorator can be applied without parentheses.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1750) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1751)         .. versionchanged:: 8.0

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1830)     def result_callback(self, replace: bool = False) -> t.Callable[[F], F]:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1831)         """Adds a result callback to the command.  By default if a
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1832)         result callback is already registered this will chain them but
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1833)         this can be disabled with the `replace` parameter.  The result
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1834)         callback is invoked with the return value of the subcommand
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1835)         (or the list of return values from all subcommands if chaining
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1836)         is enabled) as well as the parameters as they would be passed
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1837)         to the main callback.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1838) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1839)         Example::
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1840) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1841)             @click.group()
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1842)             @click.option('-i', '--input', default=23)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1843)             def cli(input):
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1844)                 return 42
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1845) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1846)             @cli.result_callback()
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1847)             def process_result(result, input):
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1848)                 return result + input
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1849) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1850)         :param replace: if set to `True` an already existing result
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1851)                         callback will be removed.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1852) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1853)         .. versionchanged:: 8.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1854)             Renamed from ``resultcallback``.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1855) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1856)         .. versionadded:: 3.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1857)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1858) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1859)         def decorator(f: F) -> F:

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1865)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1972)         # In chain mode we create the contexts step by step, but after the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1973)         # base command has been invoked.  Because at that point we do not

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1978)             ctx.invoked_subcommand = "*" if args else None

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1980) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1981)             # Otherwise we make every single context and invoke them in a
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1982)             # chain.  In that case the return value to the result processor
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1983)             # is the list of all invoked subcommand's results.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1984)             contexts = []
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1985)             while args:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1986)                 cmd_name, cmd, args = self.resolve_command(ctx, args)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1987)                 assert cmd is not None
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1988)                 sub_ctx = cmd.make_context(
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1989)                     cmd_name,

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1992)                     allow_extra_args=True,

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1994)                 )
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1995)                 contexts.append(sub_ctx)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 1996)                 args, sub_ctx.args = sub_ctx.args, []

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2002)             return _process_result(rv)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2003)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2023)         # place.

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2028)         return cmd_name if cmd else None, cmd, args[1:]

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2125) class Parameter(ABC):
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2126)     r"""A parameter to a command comes in two versions: they are either
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2127)     :class:`Option`\s or :class:`Argument`\s.  Other subclasses are currently
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2128)     not supported by design as some of the internals for parsing are
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2129)     intentionally not finalized.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2130) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2131)     Some settings are supported by both options and arguments.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2132) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2133)     :param param_decls: the parameter declarations for this option or
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2134)                         argument.  This is a list of flags or argument
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2135)                         names.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2136)     :param type: the type that should be used.  Either a :class:`ParamType`
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2137)                  or a Python type.  The latter is converted into the former
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2138)                  automatically if supported.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2139)     :param required: controls if this is optional or not.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2140)     :param default: the default value if omitted.  This can also be a callable,
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2141)                     in which case it's invoked when the default is needed
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2142)                     without any arguments.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2143)     :param callback: A function to further process or validate the value
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2144)         after type conversion. It is called as ``f(ctx, param, value)``
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2145)         and must return the value. It is called for all sources,
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2146)         including prompts.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2147)     :param nargs: the number of arguments to match.  If not ``1`` the return
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2148)                   value is a tuple instead of single value.  The default for
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2149)                   nargs is ``1`` (except if the type is a tuple, then it's
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2150)                   the arity of the tuple). If ``nargs=-1``, all remaining
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2151)                   parameters are collected.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2152)     :param metavar: how the value is represented in the help page.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2153)     :param expose_value: if this is `True` then the value is passed onwards
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2154)                          to the command callback and stored on the context,
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2155)                          otherwise it's skipped.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2156)     :param is_eager: eager values are processed before non eager ones.  This
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2157)                      should not be set for arguments or it will inverse the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2158)                      order of processing.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2159)     :param envvar: environment variable(s) that are used to provide a default value for
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2160)         this parameter. This can be a string or a sequence of strings. If a sequence is
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2161)         given, only the first non-empty environment variable is used for the parameter.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2162)     :param shell_complete: A function that returns custom shell
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2163)         completions. Used instead of the param's type completion if
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2164)         given. Takes ``ctx, param, incomplete`` and must return a list
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2165)         of :class:`~click.shell_completion.CompletionItem` or a list of
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2166)         strings.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2167)     :param deprecated: If ``True`` or non-empty string, issues a message
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2168)                         indicating that the argument is deprecated and highlights
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2169)                         its deprecation in --help. The message can be customized
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2170)                         by using a string as the value. A deprecated parameter
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2171)                         cannot be required, a ValueError will be raised otherwise.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2172) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2173)     .. versionchanged:: 8.2.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2174)         Introduction of ``deprecated``.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2175) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2176)     .. versionchanged:: 8.2
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2177)         Adding duplicate parameter names to a :class:`~click.core.Command` will
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2178)         result in a ``UserWarning`` being shown.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2179) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2180)     .. versionchanged:: 8.2
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2181)         Adding duplicate parameter names to a :class:`~click.core.Command` will
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2182)         result in a ``UserWarning`` being shown.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2183) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2184)     .. versionchanged:: 8.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2185)         ``process_value`` validates required parameters and bounded
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2186)         ``nargs``, and invokes the parameter callback before returning
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2187)         the value. This allows the callback to validate prompts.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2188)         ``full_process_value`` is removed.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2189) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2190)     .. versionchanged:: 8.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2191)         ``autocompletion`` is renamed to ``shell_complete`` and has new
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2192)         semantics described above. The old name is deprecated and will
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2193)         be removed in 8.1, until then it will be wrapped to match the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2194)         new requirements.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2195) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2196)     .. versionchanged:: 8.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2197)         For ``multiple=True, nargs>1``, the default must be a list of
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2198)         tuples.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2199) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2200)     .. versionchanged:: 8.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2201)         Setting a default is no longer required for ``nargs>1``, it will
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2202)         default to ``None``. ``multiple=True`` or ``nargs=-1`` will
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2203)         default to ``()``.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2204) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2205)     .. versionchanged:: 7.1
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2206)         Empty environment variables are ignored rather than taking the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2207)         empty string value. This makes it possible for scripts to clear
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2208)         variables if they can't unset them.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2209) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2210)     .. versionchanged:: 2.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2211)         Changed signature for parameter callback to also be passed the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2212)         parameter. The old callback format will still work, but it will
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2213)         raise a warning to give you a chance to migrate the code easier.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2214)     """

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2312)         """Gather information that could be useful for a tool generating

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2323)         return {
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2324)             "name": self.name,
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2325)             "param_type_name": self.param_type_name,
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2326)             "opts": self.opts,
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2327)             "secondary_opts": self.secondary_opts,
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2328)             "type": self.type.to_info_dict(),

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2381)         """Get the default for the parameter. Tries
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2382)         :meth:`Context.lookup_default` first, then the local default.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2383) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2384)         :param ctx: Current context.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2385)         :param call: If the default is a callable, call it. Disable to
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2386)             return the callable instead.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2387) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2388)         .. versionchanged:: 8.0.2
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2389)             Type casting is no longer performed when getting a default.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2390) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2391)         .. versionchanged:: 8.0.1
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2392)             Type casting can fail in resilient parsing mode. Invalid
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2393)             defaults will not prevent showing help text.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2394) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2395)         .. versionchanged:: 8.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2396)             Looks at ``ctx.default_map`` first.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2397) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2398)         .. versionchanged:: 8.0
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2399)             Added the ``call`` parameter.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2400)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2401)         value = ctx.lookup_default(self.name, call=False)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2402) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2403)         if value is None and not ctx._default_map_has(self.name):
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2404)             value = self.default
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2405) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2406)         if call and callable(value):
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2407)             value = value()
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2408) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2409)         return value

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2518)     def value_is_missing(self, value: t.Any) -> bool:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2519)         """A value is considered missing if:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2520) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2521)         - it is :attr:`UNSET`,

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2524)           set).
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2525) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2526)         :meta private:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2527)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2528)         if value is UNSET:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2529)             return True
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2530)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2533)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2536)     def process_value(self, ctx: Context, value: t.Any) -> t.Any:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2537)         """Process the value of this parameter:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2538) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2539)         1. Type cast the value using :meth:`type_cast_value`.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2540)         2. Check if the value is missing (see: :meth:`value_is_missing`), and raise
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2541)            :exc:`MissingParameter` if it is required.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2542)         3. If a :attr:`callback` is set, call it to have the value replaced by the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2543)            result of the callback. If the value was not set, the callback receive
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2544)            ``None``. This keep the legacy behavior as it was before the introduction of
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2545)            the :attr:`UNSET` sentinel.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2546) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2547)         :meta private:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2548)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2549)         # shelter `type_cast_value` from ever seeing an `UNSET` value by handling the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2550)         # cases in which `UNSET` gets special treatment explicitly at this layer
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2551)         #
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2552)         # Refs:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2553)         # https://github.com/pallets/click/issues/3069
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2554)         if value is UNSET:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2555)             if self.multiple or self.nargs == -1:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2556)                 value = ()
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2557)         else:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2558)             value = self.type_cast_value(ctx, value)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2559) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2560)         if self.required and self.value_is_missing(value):
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2561)             raise MissingParameter(ctx=ctx, param=self)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2562) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2563)         if self.callback is not None:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2564)             # Legacy case: UNSET is not exposed directly to the callback, but converted
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2565)             # to None.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2566)             if value is UNSET:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2567)                 value = None
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2568) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2569)             # Search for parameters with UNSET values in the context.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2570)             unset_keys = {k: None for k, v in ctx.params.items() if v is UNSET}
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2571)             # No UNSET values, call the callback as usual.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2572)             if not unset_keys:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2573)                 value = self.callback(ctx, self, value)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2574) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2575)             # Legacy case: provide a temporarily manipulated context to the callback
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2576)             # to hide UNSET values as None.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2577)             #
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2578)             # Refs:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2579)             # https://github.com/pallets/click/issues/3136
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2580)             # https://github.com/pallets/click/pull/3137
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2581)             else:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2582)                 # Add another layer to the context stack to clearly hint that the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2583)                 # context is temporarily modified.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2584)                 with ctx:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2585)                     # Update the context parameters to replace UNSET with None.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2586)                     ctx.params.update(unset_keys)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2587)                     # Feed these fake context parameters to the callback.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2588)                     value = self.callback(ctx, self, value)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2589)                     # Restore the UNSET values in the context parameters.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2590)                     ctx.params.update(
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2591)                         {
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2592)                             k: UNSET
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2593)                             for k in unset_keys
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2594)                             # Only restore keys that are present and still None, in case
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2595)                             # the callback modified other parameters.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2596)                             if k in ctx.params and ctx.params[k] is None
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2597)                         }
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2598)                     )
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2599) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2600)         return value
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2601) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2602)     def resolve_envvar_value(self, ctx: Context) -> str | None:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2603)         """Returns the value found in the environment variable(s) attached to this
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2604)         parameter.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2605) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2606)         Environment variables values are `always returned as strings
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2607)         <https://docs.python.org/3/library/os.html#os.environ>`_.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2608) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2609)         This method returns ``None`` if:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2610) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2611)         - the :attr:`envvar` property is not set on the :class:`Parameter`,
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2612)         - the environment variable is not found in the environment,
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2613)         - the variable is found in the environment but its value is empty (i.e. the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2614)           environment variable is present but has an empty string).
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2615) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2616)         If :attr:`envvar` is setup with multiple environment variables,
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2617)         then only the first non-empty value is returned.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2618) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2619)         .. caution::
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2620) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2621)             The raw value extracted from the environment is not normalized and is
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2622)             returned as-is. Any normalization or reconciliation is performed later by
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2623)             the :class:`Parameter`'s :attr:`type`.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2624) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2625)         :meta private:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2626)         """
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2627)         if not self.envvar:
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2628)             return None
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2629) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2630)         if isinstance(self.envvar, str):
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2631)             rv = os.environ.get(self.envvar)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2632)

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2684) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2685)         with augment_usage_errors(ctx, param=self):
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2686)             value, source = self.consume_value(ctx, opts)
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2687) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2688)             # Record the source before processing so eager callbacks and type

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2715)                 # In resilient parsing mode, we do not want to fail the command if the
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2716)                 # value is incompatible with the parameter type, so we reset the value
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2717)                 # to UNSET, which will be interpreted as a missing value.
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2718)                 value = UNSET
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2719) 
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2720)         # Arbitrate the slot when several parameters target the same variable

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2722)         slot_empty = existing_value is UNSET
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2723)         more_explicit = existing_source is not None and source < existing_source

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2726)             same_source
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2727)             and source == ParameterSource.DEFAULT
^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2728)             and existing_default_explicit

^c7c87e5 (evals 2026-10-22 10:53:58 +0200 2730)         )
